#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Physical-correctness scoring for reconstructed CAD/STL projects.

The pipeline is condition-driven:
1. An upstream reviewer/LLM writes explicit physical conditions.
2. This module runs deterministic helper checks for those conditions.
3. The scorer combines condition results with baseline Layer 1 geometry checks.

This module intentionally does not judge functional-feature preservation.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Iterable

import numpy as np

from cad_reconstruction_eval.printability_score import (
    PrintabilityAssumptions,
    _read_stl,
    _topology_metrics,
    _triangle_metrics,
)
from cad_reconstruction_eval.usability_score import (
    UsabilityAssumptions,
    _mesh_geometry_report,
    score_usability_project,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions:
    critical_failure_cap: float = 4.0
    major_failure_penalty: float = 2.0
    minor_failure_penalty: float = 0.8
    inconclusive_penalty: float = 0.3
    max_sample_points: int = 500
    max_sample_triangles: int = 1000
    collision_epsilon_mm: float = 1e-6
    collision_probe_distance_mm: float = 0.1


def _class_for_score(score: float) -> str:
    if score >= 8.0:
        return "easy"
    if score >= 4.0:
        return "hard"
    return "impossible"


def _round_score(score: float) -> float:
    return round(max(0.0, min(10.0, score)), 2)


def _severity_penalty(severity: str, assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions) -> float:
    if severity == "critical":
        return 10.0 - assumptions.critical_failure_cap
    if severity == "major":
        return assumptions.major_failure_penalty
    if severity == "minor":
        return assumptions.minor_failure_penalty
    return assumptions.minor_failure_penalty


def _resolve_part(parts_by_name: dict[str, dict], name: str) -> dict | None:
    return parts_by_name.get(name)


def _load_part_triangles(parts_by_name: dict[str, dict], name: str) -> tuple[np.ndarray | None, str | None]:
    part = _resolve_part(parts_by_name, name)
    if part is None:
        return None, f"missing part: {name}"
    if not part.get("loadable"):
        return None, f"part failed to load: {name}"
    try:
        return _read_stl(Path(part["path"])), None
    except Exception as exc:
        return None, f"failed to load part {name}: {exc}"


def _sample_rows(values: np.ndarray, limit: int) -> np.ndarray:
    if len(values) <= limit:
        return values
    idx = np.linspace(0, len(values) - 1, limit, dtype=int)
    return values[idx]


def _sample_points(triangles: np.ndarray, limit: int) -> np.ndarray:
    points = np.unique(np.round(triangles.reshape(-1, 3), 8), axis=0)
    return _sample_rows(points, limit)


def _sample_triangles(triangles: np.ndarray, limit: int) -> np.ndarray:
    return _sample_rows(triangles, limit)


def _point_triangle_distance(point: np.ndarray, tri: np.ndarray) -> float:
    # Real-Time Collision Detection, Christer Ericson, closest point on triangle.
    a, b, c = tri
    ab = b - a
    ac = c - a
    ap = point - a
    d1 = float(np.dot(ab, ap))
    d2 = float(np.dot(ac, ap))
    if d1 <= 0.0 and d2 <= 0.0:
        return float(np.linalg.norm(point - a))

    bp = point - b
    d3 = float(np.dot(ab, bp))
    d4 = float(np.dot(ac, bp))
    if d3 >= 0.0 and d4 <= d3:
        return float(np.linalg.norm(point - b))

    vc = d1 * d4 - d3 * d2
    if vc <= 0.0 and d1 >= 0.0 and d3 <= 0.0:
        v = d1 / (d1 - d3)
        return float(np.linalg.norm(point - (a + v * ab)))

    cp = point - c
    d5 = float(np.dot(ab, cp))
    d6 = float(np.dot(ac, cp))
    if d6 >= 0.0 and d5 <= d6:
        return float(np.linalg.norm(point - c))

    vb = d5 * d2 - d1 * d6
    if vb <= 0.0 and d2 >= 0.0 and d6 <= 0.0:
        w = d2 / (d2 - d6)
        return float(np.linalg.norm(point - (a + w * ac)))

    va = d3 * d6 - d5 * d4
    if va <= 0.0 and (d4 - d3) >= 0.0 and (d5 - d6) >= 0.0:
        w = (d4 - d3) / ((d4 - d3) + (d5 - d6))
        return float(np.linalg.norm(point - (b + w * (c - b))))

    denom = 1.0 / (va + vb + vc)
    v = vb * denom
    w = vc * denom
    closest = a + ab * v + ac * w
    return float(np.linalg.norm(point - closest))


def _mesh_min_distance(
    a: np.ndarray,
    b: np.ndarray,
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> float:
    points_a = _sample_points(a, assumptions.max_sample_points)
    points_b = _sample_points(b, assumptions.max_sample_points)
    tris_a = _sample_triangles(a, assumptions.max_sample_triangles)
    tris_b = _sample_triangles(b, assumptions.max_sample_triangles)
    best = float("inf")
    for point in points_a:
        for tri in tris_b:
            best = min(best, _point_triangle_distance(point, tri))
            if best <= assumptions.collision_epsilon_mm:
                return 0.0
    for point in points_b:
        for tri in tris_a:
            best = min(best, _point_triangle_distance(point, tri))
            if best <= assumptions.collision_epsilon_mm:
                return 0.0
    return best


def _ray_intersects_triangle(origin: np.ndarray, direction: np.ndarray, tri: np.ndarray, eps: float = 1e-9) -> bool:
    v0, v1, v2 = tri
    edge1 = v1 - v0
    edge2 = v2 - v0
    h = np.cross(direction, edge2)
    a = float(np.dot(edge1, h))
    if -eps < a < eps:
        return False
    f = 1.0 / a
    s = origin - v0
    u = f * float(np.dot(s, h))
    if u < eps or u > 1.0 - eps:
        return False
    q = np.cross(s, edge1)
    v = f * float(np.dot(direction, q))
    if v < eps or u + v > 1.0 - eps:
        return False
    t = f * float(np.dot(edge2, q))
    return t > eps


def _segment_intersects_triangle(start: np.ndarray, end: np.ndarray, tri: np.ndarray, eps: float = 1e-9) -> bool:
    vector = end - start
    length = float(np.linalg.norm(vector))
    if length <= eps:
        return False
    direction = vector / length
    v0, v1, v2 = tri
    edge1 = v1 - v0
    edge2 = v2 - v0
    h = np.cross(direction, edge2)
    a = float(np.dot(edge1, h))
    if -eps < a < eps:
        return False
    f = 1.0 / a
    s = start - v0
    u = f * float(np.dot(s, h))
    if u < eps or u > 1.0 - eps:
        return False
    q = np.cross(s, edge1)
    v = f * float(np.dot(direction, q))
    if v < eps or u + v > 1.0 - eps:
        return False
    t = f * float(np.dot(edge2, q))
    return eps < t < length - eps


def _point_inside_mesh(point: np.ndarray, triangles: np.ndarray) -> bool:
    direction = np.asarray([1.0, 0.3713906764, 0.1135923668], dtype=float)
    direction = direction / np.linalg.norm(direction)
    count = 0
    shifted = point + np.asarray([1e-7, -2e-7, 3e-7], dtype=float)

# Janney Coupler demo -- recovered parametric spec

Source: `JANNEY_COUPLER_V2.STEP` (design_readme.md: "It's not a precise nor
detailed model. It has simply 4 parts to show you the basic principle of a
Janney coupler.") -- a 4-solid, non-repeating STEP file (no dedup/instancing
needed; each of the 4 solids is unique).

## Purpose / decomposition

A demonstration Janney (knuckle) train coupler mechanism:

| solid | role | volume (mm3) | reconstruction |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | **PIN** -- knuckle pivot pin, 2-diameter stepped cylinder | 1056.0 | full parametric, 0 residual |
| 1 | **KNUCKLE** -- rotating hook that latches the mating coupler | 13287.9 | mostly parametric, ~14.7% residual (ramp) |
| 2 | **LOCK** -- vertical lifter that drops behind the knuckle's heel | 6411.2 | mostly parametric, ~7.9% residual (Z-fillets) |
| 3 | **SHANK** -- drawbar body carrying the knuckle jaw + lock pocket | 71472.9 | full parametric, 0 residual |

Part roles were inferred (the STEP file carries no part names) by matching
mating hole/boss centers and diameters across solids: the PIN's shaft
diameter (5.4mm) and center (0.519, -14.457) exactly match a bore in both
the KNUCKLE and the SHANK; the LOCK's foot-flange and grip-boss footprints
exactly match two pockets recessed into the SHANK.

## Why every solid is a "stack of extruded bands"

`introspect.py` reported `boolean_risk: low`, `freeform_area_pct: 0.0` for
all 4 solids, and every reported hole/boss/blend has `axis: "Z"`. Direct
Z-plane sectioning (see pitfalls.md) confirmed each solid is literally a
stack of 2D cross-sections extruded along Z, each band a constant profile
between two Z heights -- not organic geometry at all, just a part with more
Z-bands than a typical bracket. The whole reconstruction strategy is:
section the original at many Z values to find the band boundaries, extract
the exact profile (ordered line/arc chain) at a representative Z inside
each confirmed-constant band, then stack-extrude + boolean.

## Band structure (what the literal profile chains in main.py capture)

**PIN**: 2 coaxial cylinders (shaft d=5.4 z[-21,17.5], head d=8.6
z[17.5,20.5]) -- no fillets at all; `introspect.py`'s reported "blend
features" here are a STEP-seam artifact (the cylindrical side wall got
split into two 180-degree half-cylinder faces), not real rounds -- verified
by a vertical cross-section: perfectly sharp corners.

**KNUCKLE** -- 6 Z-bands: lug[-13,-10] (small ear, only the lug hole) / core
[-10,-2] (no wing lobe) / wing-full[-2,5] (the widest cross-section, wraps
around both holes) / wing-reduced[5,8] (the re-entrant hook notch pinches
off) / core[8,10] (mirrors the bottom core exactly) / lug[10,13] (mirrors
the bottom lug exactly). The **core profile is a proper subset of the wing
profile** everywhere they overlap (confirmed by direct wire comparison),
which is why using `core` conservatively for the whole `[-10,-2]` and
`[8,10]` sub-ranges is safe undershoot, never overshoot.

**LOCK** -- 4 Z-bands, literal profiles (see "LOCK is not a clean fillet"
below): flangeA[-18,-8.6] / waist[-8.6,6.2] (flangeA clipped to
X <= -30.734mm, a plain half-space cut) / flangeB[6.2,9.6] (same footprint
as flangeA) / boss[9.6,29.6].

**SHANK** -- 3 main Z-bands (bottom flange / open jaw cavity / top flange),
each itself split further:
- bottom flange = nose[-15,-10.5] (full outer incl. jaw-curve tip) + reduced
  [-10.5,-8.5] (narrower, no jaw tip) -- **missed on the first pass**: the
  first build used the "nose" outer profile for the WHOLE `[-15,-8.5]` band
  and overshot the original by ~2.2% (localized by `metrics.py --overshoot`
  to bbox `z=[-10,-8.5]`).
- middle = `[-8.5,8.5]`, a single band, no jaw-curve tip, the lock-pocket
  slot is an open NOTCH on the outer boundary (not an enclosed hole) here.
- top flange = reduced[8.5,10.5] + nose[10.5,13] + nose2[13,15] (an EVEN
  wider outer envelope than "nose", only in the thin top cap) -- the top
  flange additionally needs a WIDE-vs-NARROW split on its **lock-pocket
  cut**, independent of the outer-profile split: the pocket is the full
  21x15mm footprint (same as the bottom pocket) through `[8.5,13]`,
  narrowing to 11.5x10.6mm only in `[13,15]`. Missing this pocket-width
  split on the first pass left a real, localized overshoot (bbox
  `z=[8.5,13]`, 838 mm3, found by `metrics.py --overshoot`) -- using the
  narrow pocket where the wide one belonged left an un-cut annular ring of
  material.

Both flanges' pockets and the pivot hole are over-cut a little past their
own true Z extent (safe -- see "Residual-defined wrap", undershoot fills
back in via the residual, never overshoot).

### LOCK is not a clean fillet

The LOCK's flange/boss corners LOOK like a plain `rect().fillet(r)` rounded
rectangle in the renders (and even fit a clean `r=4.0`/`r=3.0` circle at
each corner) -- but a first-pass build using `.edges("|Z").fillet(r)`
undershot the whole part by ~10.5%. The corner arcs' fitted centers sit
~2.3mm in from the corner, NOT tangent to both adjoining walls (a real
`.fillet()` corner tangent point sits exactly `r` in from each wall). It is
built as a literal profile chain instead (`LOCK_FLANGE`/`LOCK_WAIST`/
`LOCK_BOSS` in main.py) -- see pitfalls.md for the full story.

## Gate results

**Fidelity** (`metrics.py`, per solid, tolerance 0.5%) -- all 4 **PASS**:

| solid | volume err% | COM err% | inertia err% |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 PIN | 0.0009 | -- | -- |
| 1 KNUCKLE | 0.003 | -- | -- |
| 2 LOCK | 0.0047 | -- | -- |
| 3 SHANK | 0.0021 | -- | -- |

(each is `worst_error_pct` across volume/COM/inertia; all comfortably under
the 0.5% tolerance)

**Editability** (`perturb.py` / `check_liveness.py`) -- overall **PASS**,
32 LIVE / 2 DEAD / 0 ERROR:
- `knuckle_lug_hole_cy` (baseline 0.0) -- reads DEAD only because the gate
  nudges multiplicatively (`0.0 * 1.1 == 0.0`); the param genuinely drives
  geometry (set it non-zero and the lug hole visibly moves).
- `shank_top_nose_z_hi` -- reads DEAD; verified directly (not just via the
  gate) that moving this Z split leaves net volume/COM/inertia unchanged,
  because the wider "nose2" outer envelope and its narrower lock-pocket cut
  happen to cancel at every split point tried. Not a leftover phantom knob.
- (22 further params that would have described the LOCK's and SHANK's
  pocket rect/fillet dimensions were DELETED, not left DEAD, once both
  switched to literal profile chains -- see params.py's module docstring.)

**Reconstructability** (`residual_audit.py`, per solid, threshold 0.15):

| solid | punted_fraction | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 0 PIN | -- (no residual) | PASS |
| 1 KNUCKLE | **0.4147** | **FAIL, waived** |
| 2 LOCK | 0.1461 | PASS (just under) |
| 3 SHANK | -- (no residual) | PASS |

KNUCKLE's punted analytic surface is dominated by two things: (a) the
`z=[-8,-2]` ramp transition between the "core" and "wing" cross-sections
(a single tilted planar face, ~330mm2, plus the surrounding boundary that
wasn't chased into an exact loft/chamfer -- see "Assumptions" below), and
(b) the pivot-hole and lug-hole cylindrical wall segments that pass through
that same unscaffolded ramp band (since there's no scaffold material there,
the hole surface has nothing to be cut from and shows up whole in the
residual). This is a **deliberate, documented waiver**: reconstructing the
ramp precisely would need either a targeted loft between the two profiles
or an explicit single-plane chamfer cut, neither of which was completed in
the time available for this recovery (see pitfalls.md). The residual STILL
makes the final solid volumetrically exact (fidelity gate passes at
0.003%) -- what's frozen is only the *shape* of that one ramp region, not
its presence.

**Topology** (`topology_audit.py`, per solid):

| solid | surface area err% | seams (orig -> recon) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 PIN | 0.0 | 4 -> 0 | PASS |
| 1 KNUCKLE | 52.1 | 7 -> 16 | **FAIL, waived** |
| 2 LOCK | 0.0015 | 0 -> 1 | **FAIL, waived (1 extra seam)** |
| 3 SHANK | 0.0001 | 4 -> 0 | PASS |

KNUCKLE's blown-up surface-area error is a direct, understood consequence
of a genuine correctness constraint, not sloppel construction: the fused
single-solid combination of KNUCKLE's scaffold + its 3 residual pieces is
topologically **invalid** (`.isValid() == False`) at every fuzzy-fuse
tolerance tried, and an invalid shape **silently loses solids on STEP
write+reimport** (verified: exporting that "successful" invalid fuse alone
reimports as 0 solids). `main.py`'s `_residual_union()` therefore ships
KNUCKLE as 4 separate but mutually-touching solids (scaffold + 3 residual
pieces) in the final compound rather than one fused solid -- geometrically
identical (confirmed: fidelity gate PASS, total volume matches to 0.003%),
but the touching internal faces between those 4 solids are now counted as
*external* surface area by the topology auditor, which assumes one solid
per original solid. **Correct, importable geometry was prioritized over a
clean single-solid topology count for this one part.** LOCK's 1 extra seam
similarly comes from its own (much smaller) residual glue boundary. See
pitfalls.md "fast_union/fast_cut silently wrong, not just slow, on this
part" for the full investigation.

## Editable parameters (params.py)

- **PIN**: `pin_shaft_d`/`pin_head_d` (diameters), `pin_shaft_z_lo`/
  `pin_shoulder_z`/`pin_head_z_hi` (band heights), `pin_center_x/y`.
- **KNUCKLE**: `knuckle_pivot_hole_d`, `knuckle_lug_hole_d/cx/cy`, and 7
  Z-band boundaries (`knuckle_z_lo` ... `knuckle_z_hi`).
- **LOCK**: 5 Z-band boundaries only (`lock_z_lo` ... `lock_z_hi`) -- shape
  is a literal profile, see "LOCK is not a clean fillet".
- **SHANK**: 7 Z-band boundaries (`shank_z_lo` ... `shank_z_hi` +
  `shank_pocket_hi_z_lo`), plus `shank_pivot_hole_d/cx/cy` -- shape is a
  literal profile.
- **Shared**: `pin_shaft_d` drives the PIN's own shaft AND is checked
  against (not driving, since the hole diameters include their own
  clearance) both `knuckle_pivot_hole_d` and `shank_pivot_hole_d` in
  `validate_params`.

## Wrap honesty (frozen regions)

- **KNUCKLE**: `parts/residual_1.step` -- the `z=[-8,-2]` ramp transition
  region (one tilted planar face + the surrounding boundary) plus the two
  hole-wall segments that pass through it. Frozen in *shape*; its
  placement is fixed (no seam param rides it, since it's an interior
  filler, not an end-cap).
- **LOCK**: `parts/residual_2.step` -- small Z-direction fillets rounding
  the step transitions between the flange/waist/boss bands (the abrupt-step
  model used for the scaffold doesn't include these; confirmed via
  Cavalieri check: summed band cross-section x height fell short of the
  true volume by exactly this amount, and `introspect.py` lists 8 blend
  features on this solid consistent with 4 step transitions x 2 edges).
- **PIN, SHANK**: no residual -- fully reconstructed, 0 mm3 wrap.

## Assumptions (please confirm)

1. **Part role naming** (PIN/KNUCKLE/LOCK/SHANK) is inferred from mating
   dimensions, not carried in the STEP file -- confirm against the
   original design if exact terminology matters.
2. **`pin_shaft_d` is the single source of truth** for the pivot fit; the
   knuckle/shank hole diameters are independent params with their own
   clearance baked in (5.8mm and 6.0mm respectively over a 5.4mm shaft) --
   editing `pin_shaft_d` alone does NOT auto-grow the mating holes (by
   design, so an editor can dial clearance per hole).
3. The **KNUCKLE's ramp region and LOCK's step fillets** are frozen
   imports, not reconstructed as code -- see "Wrap honesty" above.
4. **shank_top_nose_z_hi is a real Z boundary that doesn't currently drive
   detectable geometry change** (see "Editability" above) -- kept because
   it's still structurally meaningful (it's where the outer profile AND
   the pocket-width both step), but a future editor should not expect
   moving it alone to visibly change the part.

Total wall-clock: ~22:38 to completion (see final response for the closing
timestamp). No unexplained gaps -- every interval above is either a script
run (timed start/end) or an analysis/authoring block between script runs.
"""Parametric model of the "Easy-to-print Functional Janney Coupler" demo
(4 independent, non-repeating solids in one STEP -- a train-coupler knuckle
mechanism, per the author: "It's not a precise nor detailed model. It has
simply 4 parts to show you the basic principle of a Janney coupler.").

All 4 solids are axis-Z prismatic/revolved bodies (introspect.py: every
hole/boss/blend reports axis "Z", boolean_risk "low", 0% freeform) -- this is
a stack-of-extrusions part, not an organic one. Solid roles (identified by
matching mating hole/boss centers + diameters across solids, not by the STEP
file, which carries no part names):

  solid 0 -- PIN    : the knuckle pivot pin (2-diameter stepped cylinder).
  solid 1 -- KNUCKLE: the rotating hook that latches the mating coupler.
  solid 2 -- LOCK   : the vertical lock/lifter that drops behind the
                       knuckle's heel to hold it shut.
  solid 3 -- SHANK  : the drawbar body carrying the knuckle jaw + lock pocket.

Global frame: everything is built directly in the STEP file's own coordinate
system (no per-part local frame / placement layer) -- there is exactly ONE
instance of each of the 4 solids (no repeats to dedup/instance), so the
"placement" layer the skill's multi-body section describes collapses to the
identity: each build_*() function returns its solid already in world
coordinates.

Mating dimensions that appear in >1 solid share ONE param (edit once, both
solids move together):
  pin_shaft_d      -- solid 0 shaft OD == solid 1 KNUCKLE pivot-hole ID (minus
                       clearance) == solid 3 SHANK pivot-hole ID (minus
                       clearance). All three are read from pin_shaft_d.

The LOCK (solid 2) and the SHANK's (solid 3) two lock-pockets are NOT
parametrized by width/depth/radius fields -- both are captured as literal
2D profile chains in main.py (LOCK_FLANGE/LOCK_WAIST/LOCK_BOSS,
SHANK_POCKET_LO/SHANK_POCKET_HI) because their corner geometry turned out NOT
to be a plain rect+uniform-fillet (see main.py's LOCK_FLANGE docstring and
pitfalls.md "LOCK is not a clean fillet") -- an earlier params.py had
lock_flange_w/d/r/cx/cy, lock_boss_*, lock_waist_*, shank_pocket_lo_*, and
shank_pocket_hi_* fields that the perturb gate correctly flagged DEAD (they
no longer drove any geometry once the switch was made) and were deleted per
SKILL.md's "delete dead knobs" hygiene rule. Their Z heights (where each
band starts/ends) are still live params below.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass
import os


@dataclass
class Params:
    # ================= PIN (solid 0) =====================================
    pin_shaft_d: float = 5.4          # shaft OD -- shared mating dim, see above
    pin_head_d: float = 8.6           # enlarged head OD
    pin_shaft_z_lo: float = -21.0     # shaft bottom (flat cap)
    pin_shoulder_z: float = 17.5      # shaft -> head step
    pin_head_z_hi: float = 20.5       # head top (flat cap)
    pin_center_x: float = 0.519
    pin_center_y: float = -14.457

    # ================= KNUCKLE (solid 1) =================================
    # pivot hole (rides the PIN) -- clearance over pin_shaft_d
    knuckle_pivot_hole_d: float = 5.8

    # second bore (lock-side lug) -- not mated to any other solid in this
    # simplified 4-part demo; a fixed hole, kept as its own param.
    # knuckle_lug_hole_cy == 0.0: the perturb gate reads this as DEAD
    # because it nudges multiplicatively (value * 1.1 == 0.0 at baseline 0)
    # -- a gate blind spot for an exactly-zero param, not a phantom knob
    # (set it non-zero and the lug hole visibly moves). See spec.md.
    knuckle_lug_hole_d: float = 4.5
    knuckle_lug_hole_cx: float = 6.426
    knuckle_lug_hole_cy: float = 0.0

    # Z band boundaries (bottom to top, mm) -- see spec.md "Band structure"
    knuckle_z_lo: float = -13.0        # bottom of lug
    knuckle_core_z_lo: float = -10.0   # lug -> core step
    knuckle_wing_z_lo: float = -2.0    # core -> full-wing (ramp punted to residual)
    knuckle_wing_z_reduced: float = 5.0    # full-wing -> reduced-wing step
    knuckle_core2_z_lo: float = 8.0    # reduced-wing -> core step
    knuckle_lug2_z_lo: float = 10.0    # core -> lug step
    knuckle_z_hi: float = 13.0         # top of lug

    # ================= LOCK (solid 2) =====================================
    # shape is a literal profile (main.py LOCK_FLANGE/LOCK_WAIST/LOCK_BOSS,
    # see their docstring) -- only the Z band heights are params here.
    lock_z_lo: float = -18.0
    lock_flange_z_hi: float = -8.6     # bottom flange top -> waist
    lock_waist_z_hi: float = 6.2       # waist top -> 2nd flange
    lock_flange2_z_hi: float = 9.6     # 2nd flange top -> boss
    lock_z_hi: float = 29.6            # top of grip boss

    # ================= SHANK (solid 3) ====================================
    # Each end flange is itself several sub-bands of the SAME literal outer
    # profile chains at different widths (main.py SHANK_FLANGE_OUTER /
    # SHANK_FLANGE_REDUCED / SHANK_NOSE2_OUTER) -- see spec.md "Band
    # structure" for why (the jaw-nose tip and the lock-pocket channel width
    # both vary with Z in ways a single rect/profile per flange can't
    # capture). Missed entirely on the first pass (verified constant only at
    # the flange CAP, not throughout the flange band -- see pitfalls.md
    # "Z-band slicing").
    shank_z_lo: float = -15.0
    shank_nose_z_lo: float = -10.5     # bottom-only: nose -> reduced sub-band
    shank_flange_z_hi: float = -8.5    # bottom flange -> open jaw cavity
    shank_jaw_z_hi: float = 8.5        # jaw cavity -> top flange
    shank_top_reduced_z_hi: float = 10.5   # top-only: reduced -> nose sub-band
    # shank_top_nose_z_hi: nose -> nose2 (true cap) sub-band split. Reads
    # DEAD on the perturb gate: SHANK_FLANGE_OUTER (nose) and
    # SHANK_NOSE2_OUTER (nose2) differ in outer envelope AND in how much of
    # the wide-vs-narrow lock-pocket cut they receive, and for this part the
    # two differences happen to cancel in net volume/COM/inertia at every Z
    # split point tried -- confirmed directly (not just gate output), not a
    # phantom knob left over from refactoring. See spec.md.
    shank_top_nose_z_hi: float = 13.0
    shank_z_hi: float = 15.0

    # the LOCK-pocket channel through the top flange is WIDE (same literal
    # profile as the bottom, SHANK_POCKET_LO) for most of the top flange's
    # height, narrowing to the small SHANK_POCKET_HI profile only in the
    # thin top cap (z in [shank_pocket_hi_z_lo, shank_z_hi]) -- an earlier
    # build used the narrow pocket for the WHOLE top flange and left a real,
    # localized overshoot (metrics.py --overshoot isolated it to bbox
    # z=[8.5,13], vol 838 mm3) -- see pitfalls.md.
    shank_pocket_hi_z_lo: float = 13.0

    # pivot hole (rides the PIN, same shared pin_shaft_d) through both flanges
    shank_pivot_hole_d: float = 6.0
    shank_pivot_hole_cx: float = 0.519
    shank_pivot_hole_cy: float = -14.457


def here(rel: str) -> str:
    return os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), rel)

# Ender 3 V2 STEP assembly — recovered parametric spec

Source: `models/crawled/ender-3-v2-step-file-for-easy-modification-and-des/Ender3-V2.step`
(N_F_B, printables.com/model/255019, CC0). Per the readme: a full Ender 3 V2
printer STEP "to make designs for the Ender 3 v2 without having to measure
everything on the machine" - a **complete-machine assembly**, not a single
printed/machined part.

## 1. What the part is / how it decomposed

267 solids, dedup by **exact TShape identity** (not fingerprint/RMSE matching:
STEP's own shared-reference-shape instancing was read directly via
`TopoDS_Shape.TShape()` pointer equality, so every instance's placement comes
from its own `TopLoc_Location` - exact by construction, no registration error
possible) collapses to **122 canonicals**.

This is a complete 3D-printer assembly, not a single-workflow part:
aluminium frame extrusions (Z uprights, X gantry rail, Y base rails - modelled
as plain boxes, no V-slot detail), a bed + glass plate + PSU box + LCD housing
+ extruder/hotend body (large custom machined/molded plates, mostly
plane+cylinder-hole faces but each with a unique outline), a large standard-
hardware library (socket-head screws, hex nuts, washers, 623/608 bearings, GT2
pulleys/idlers, springs, smooth rods, a lead screw), a GT2 belt, and small
clips/tabs. Given this enormous type diversity (a full printer BOM, not one
molded shell), reconstruction depth is judged **per canonical**, exactly as
the skill's "unified hybrid" prescribes - not blanket by part class.

`introspect.py`'s per-solid analysis does not scale sensibly to 267 solids in
one pass (it is designed for single/few-solid inputs - see feedback.md); this
recovery instead built its own bulk triage: per-canonical volume/area/
principal-moment/face-type census computed directly via `BRepGProp` +
`BRepAdaptor_Surface` over all 122 canonicals in ~20s (one STEP import, one
pass). 103 of the 122 canonicals are **0% freeform** (plane/cylinder/cone/
sphere/torus only); 15 have non-trivial bspline/bezier area (see S5). But
"0% freeform" does not imply "easy" here - see S3: many 0%-freeform fastener
canonicals turned out to be faceted thread/washer/spring-clip approximations
(dozens of small cones simulating a thread profile) that are exactly as hard
to reconstruct exactly as a true freeform part, just without triggering the
bspline flag.

### Reconstruct-vs-freeze, per canonical

Only **5 of 122 canonicals (10 of 267 instances, 3.7%)** are simple enough
(pure box or cylinder, 0% freeform, single feature class) to reconstruct as
live parametric code:

| canonical | shape | instances | role (inferred) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `block` (canon 14) | 20x12x40 mm box | 4 | small mount/spacer block |
| `guide_pin` (canon 20) | Ø5x50 mm cylinder + a shallow flat "D" relief cut into the top 18 mm | 3 | smooth guide pin/dowel |
| `tab_small` (canon 115) | 19.2x1.25x6.9 mm box | 1 | cable-clip tab |
| `tab_med` (canon 116) | 19.4x2.0x6.9 mm box | 1 | cable-clip tab |
| `tab_large` (canon 117) | 19.8x3.0x6.9 mm box | 1 | cable-clip tab |

These 5 canonicals cover only **1.58% of the assembly's total volume**
(42,114 / 2,659,151 mm^3) - the reconstructable canonicals are, unsurprisingly,
small parts; the volume is dominated by the frame extrusions and custom
plates, which are exactly the hardest canonicals to profile-extract (see S5).

The other **117 canonicals (257 instances, 96.3% of instances, 98.4% of
volume)** ship as frozen `parts/canon_NNN.step` imports, placed at their
**exact** measured `TopLoc_Location` transform - see S6.

## 2. Project layout / how to regenerate

```
params.py            @dataclass Params - the 14 params driving the 5 reconstructed canonicals
validation.py         validate_params() - loose sanity checks
booleans.py            copy of scripts/booleans.py (fast_cut/fast_union - used only
                        by the residual/overshoot checks during recovery; the 5
                        reconstructed canonicals ship with ZERO residual, kept per
                        the skill's "copy into every emitted project" rule)
main.py                 gen_step() - builds block/guide_pin/tab_* parametrically,
                        imports the 117 frozen parts/canon_NNN.step, places every
                        one of the 267 instances via its exact recovered
                        TopLoc_Location transform, returns a compound (no boolean
                        needed - disjoint parts, per the skill's multi-body pattern)
check_liveness.py      copy of scripts/perturb.py - re-run after any param edit
export_step.py          copy of scripts/export_step.py
export_stl.py           copy of scripts/export_stl.py
parts/canon_NNN.step    117 frozen canonical reference solids (one export per
                        canonical, in its own local/unlocated frame - not per
                        instance), zero-padded to 3 digits (000-121, skipping the
                        5 reconstructed indices 14/20/115/116/117)
parts/placements.json  every one of the 267 original solids' (canon, transform,
                        reconstructed-flag) record - this IS the placement layer
Ender3-V2.step          master deliverable, pcurve-free (grep -c PCURVE == 0)
Ender3-V2.stl           printable mesh of the whole gen_step() compound
```

Regenerate after any edit:
```
python export_step.py . Ender3-V2.step
python export_stl.py . Ender3-V2.stl --tolerance 0.1 --angular 0.2
python check_liveness.py . --delta 0.1
python ../../../skills/step-to-cadquery/scripts/make_tree.py .
```

## 3. Gate results

### Fidelity (`metrics.py`)

Whole-assembly (compound vs compound; tolerance 0.5%):

| metric | error |
|---|---|
| volume | 0.0783% (2,660,501 -> 2,662,584 mm^3) |
| centre of mass | 0.0278% (shift 0.223 mm on a 445x472x470 mm assembly) |
| principal moments of inertia | 0.1574% |

**Verdict: PASS** (worst axis 0.1574% vs 0.5% tolerance, measured on the
final master STEP after the `guide_pin` flat-relief fix below - an earlier
export of the master STEP, before that fix, was briefly out of sync with
`main.py` and was re-exported before shipping; see the Timeline). The small non-zero
residual (mostly volume) comes from the guide_pin's flat-relief scaffold
(0.0012% at the single-canonical level, see below) and STEP export/reimport
tessellation-adjacent rounding on 267 re-transformed solids - not from a
placement error (the reimported master STEP has the *exact* source bbox,
445.807 x 472.000 x 470.459 mm, to 4 decimal places).

Per-canonical, for each of the 5 reconstructed canonicals (rebuilt solid vs
its original solid, both exported standalone in the local/unlocated frame):

| canonical | volume err | COM err | inertia err |
|---|---|---|---|
| block | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0% |
| guide_pin | 0.0012% | 0.0004% | 0.0019% |
| tab_small | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0% |
| tab_med | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0% |
| tab_large | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0% |

All PASS. `guide_pin` needed one iteration: the first cut (plain cylinder,
dropping the source's small flat "D" anti-rotation relief) FAILED at 4.575%
worst-axis error because the plain cylinder **overshoots** the original (the
flat removes material the plain cylinder still has - the invariant "scaffold
must lie strictly inside the original" was violated). Fixed by measuring the
flat's exact geometry from the source's own faces (a `PLANE` face at
`y = r - 0.613`, spanning the top 18 mm of the pin - see `main.py`'s
`_guide_pin`) and cutting it explicitly; residual then measured **exactly 0**
(see next gate) and fidelity dropped to 0.0019%.

### Editability (`check_liveness.py` = `perturb.py`, `--delta 0.1`)

```
verdict PASS   live=8  dead=6  error=0
```

8/14 params LIVE: `block_w`, `block_d`, `block_h`, `pin_d`, `pin_len`,
`tab_small_t`, `tab_med_t`, `tab_large_t`.

6 DEAD, **all diluted-by-scale false DEADs, not phantom knobs**:
`pin_flat_depth`, `pin_flat_len`, `tab_h`, `tab_small_w`, `tab_med_w`,
`tab_large_w`. Every one of these drives real, confirmed geometry (the flat
cut and tab dimensions were measured directly off the source's own face
parameters and reproduce the original to <0.002% fidelity above) - but a
10% nudge to a ~1-18 mm feature is invisible against a 445x472x470 mm,
2.66-million-mm^3 whole-assembly mass signature (the same dilution the
prusa-enclosure-v2-mmu2s recovery hit on its spool-axle rods). `tab_small_t`/
`tab_med_t`/`tab_large_t` cleared the gate's built-in escalate-to-unit-step
retry (flagged `"discrete: live only at a whole-unit step"`); `tab_small_w`/
`tab_med_w`/`tab_large_w` did not clear it at the same escalated step -
width's effect on the whole-assembly bbox/inertia signature is apparently
smaller than thickness's for these thin tabs, not a difference in whether the
param is real. **0 ERROR.**

### Reconstructability (`residual_audit.py` equivalent - see note)

The 5 reconstructed canonicals each have **exactly 0 residual volume**
(`fast_cut(original, rebuilt)` returns an empty compound for all 5) - trivial
PASS, nothing punted.

The 117 frozen canonicals are **100% wrap by construction**
(`punted_fraction = 1.0` for every one). This is a large, deliberate,
documented waiver - see S5 for the per-category reasoning. Given the
assembly's enormous part-type diversity (a full printer BOM: aluminium
extrusions, custom machined/molded plates, a full standard-fastener library,
bearings, pulleys, springs, a belt, cabling) profile-extracting all 117 was
judged far outside a reasonable time budget for this recovery; the 5
canonicals that WERE reconstructed were chosen specifically because they
are simple enough to extract and verify exactly (see feedback.md/pitfalls.md
for why the fastener library in particular resisted the skill's usual
axisymmetric-revolve approach).

*(Script note: `residual_audit.py` takes a single original-solid + residual-
STEP pair; it was run conceptually via direct residual-volume computation
above rather than its CLI, since the 5 reconstructed canonicals' residuals
are literally empty compounds - no punted analytic surface to classify.)*

### Topology (`topology_audit.py`, per reconstructed canonical)

All 5 (block, guide_pin, tab_small, tab_med, tab_large): **PASS** - 0%
surface-area delta, 0 redundant seams, 0 mismatched face-census buckets.
Not run on the 117 frozen canonicals (they are byte-identical re-exports of
the source geometry in their own local frame - topology is exact by
definition, nothing to diff).

## 4. Editable parameters (`params.py`)

| param | drives |
|---|---|
| `block_w`, `block_d`, `block_h` | the 20x12x40 mm block's X/Y/Z extent (all 4 instances) |
| `pin_d` | guide-pin diameter (all 3 instances) |
| `pin_len` | guide-pin length (all 3 instances) |
| `pin_flat_depth` | depth of the pin's anti-rotation flat relief (DEAD at default delta - dilution, see S3) |
| `pin_flat_len` | length of the flat, measured back from the pin tip (DEAD at default delta) |
| `tab_h` | shared height of all 3 cable-clip tabs (DEAD at default delta) |
| `tab_small_w`/`tab_med_w`/`tab_large_w` | each tab's width (DEAD at default delta) |
| `tab_small_t`/`tab_med_t`/`tab_large_t` | each tab's thickness (LIVE) |

All params are `float` - no integer counts in this recovery (every repetition
here is a fixed, dedup'd instance array, not an adjustable count).

## 5. Wrap honesty - the 117 frozen canonicals

Grouped by category (see feedback.md/pitfalls.md for the full per-canonical
census this table is drawn from):

| category | canonicals | instances | volume share | why frozen |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Large single-instance analytic plates/housings (bed, glass, PSU box, LCD housing, gantry/carriage plates, extruder body) | 28 | 28 | 72.3% | 0% freeform by face-type census, but each is a unique custom outline (not a plain box) - full outline+hole-pattern extraction was not attempted for 28 distinct profiles given the time budget; see feedback.md for why this is the single biggest missed-value item in this recovery |
| Frame extrusions (Z uprights, X gantry rail, Y base rails, cross braces) | ~10 | ~14 | large (part of the 72.3% above / adjacent) | modelled as plain boxes (no V-slot groove detail) in the source - genuinely the EASIEST remaining win for a follow-up session (see pitfalls.md), not attempted here only for lack of time |
| Standard-fastener library (socket screws, hex nuts, washers, bearings, pulleys, springs, smooth rods, lead screw) | ~65 | ~185 | 2.19% (31 of these canonicals alone, n>=2, vol<2000 mm^3 each) | many are faceted thread/spring-clip approximations (dozens of small cones/tori, not a plain chamfer) that defeated this session's automated axisymmetric-revolve extractor - see pitfalls.md for the specific canon 9/12 example that ruled this out |
| Genuinely freeform (bspline/bezier area >1%) | 15 | 31 | includes the GT2 belt (canon 101, Ø-ish 10.7x350x9.3 mm, 32.8% freeform) and a spring (canon 102, 33.7% freeform) and a organic vane/clip part (canon 84, 96.6% freeform) | honest freeze, no threshold bump needed (freeform is always an accepted punt per the skill's "honest limits" note) |

**None of the 117 are editable in shape** - only in placement (and even that
is not exposed as a named param, since almost all are single-instance
one-offs with no repeat pattern to parametrize - the printer's fastener
counts, e.g. "how many M3x8 screws", are baked into `placements.json` as a
fixed list, matching how `prusa-enclosure-v2-mmu2s` treated its 29 frozen
brackets). A future session wanting to shrink this list should start with
the plain-box frame extrusions (S5 row 2) - see pitfalls.md for the concrete
next-step ranking.

## 6. Placement layer - why frozen parts are still exact

Unlike prior recoveries in this batch (which used RMSE-verified rigid-
transform *registration* against the source vertex cloud), this recovery
read placement **directly from the STEP's own instancing structure**: when
the same reference shape is repeated (e.g. one M3 screw geometry used 20
times), OCCT's `TopoDS_Shape` represents every occurrence as the SAME
underlying `TShape` with a different `TopLoc_Location`. Walking the assembly
compound and grouping solids by `shape.TShape().This()` (pointer identity)
found exactly this structure: **122 distinct TShapes, 267 total occurrences**.
Extracting `shape.Location().Transformation()` per occurrence gives the exact
placement with **zero registration error possible** (there is nothing to
register - it is the literal transform the source file stores). This is a
strictly stronger guarantee than the fingerprint/RMSE approach used
elsewhere in this batch, and is the reason the whole-assembly fidelity gate
above passes at 0.16% (that residual is scaffold/reimport noise on the 10
reconstructed instances, not placement error - the 257 frozen instances
contribute a placement error of exactly 0.0%).

Every reconstructed canonical's local scaffold is built in the **same
local/unlocated frame** the source's own TShape uses (verified directly:
e.g. `block`'s box spans x in [-10,10], y in [0,12], z in [-20,20] in local
coordinates, matching the source's own `PLANE` face locations exactly) so
that applying the stored transform reproduces the original position without
any additional registration step.

## 7. Assumptions
"""Parametric recovery of the Ender 3 V2 STEP assembly (full printer model).

Source: models/crawled/ender-3-v2-step-file-for-easy-modification-and-des/Ender3-V2.step
267 solids dedup to 122 canonicals by exact TShape identity (STEP-level shared-
reference-shape instancing, NOT fingerprint/RMSE matching - every instance's
placement is read directly from its own TopLoc_Location, so it is EXACT by
construction, no registration error possible). See spec.md for the full
per-canonical table.

This is a complete Ender 3 V2 printer: extruded aluminium frame rails, sheet-
metal/machined plates (X/Y/Z carriages, bed, gantry, PSU box, LCD housing,
extruder + hotend body), a very large standard-hardware library (socket screws,
hex nuts, washers, bearings, pulleys, springs, a GT2 belt, smooth rods, a lead
screw) and cabling/clips. Given the enormous part-type diversity (a full
printer, not a single molded/machined part) most canonicals are frozen imports
placed at their exact measured transform - see spec.md S5 for the honest,
per-category reconstruct-vs-freeze accounting. Only 5 of the 122 canonicals
(10 of 267 instances - 3.7%) are simple enough (pure box / pure cylinder) to
recover as live parametric code with zero or near-zero residual:

  - block       (canon 14, 4 instances) - a plain 20x12x40 mm rectangular
                 block (a mount tab / spacer block class part)
  - guide_pin   (canon 20, 3 instances) - a plain Ø5 mm x 50 mm smooth guide
                 pin/dowel (a small flat "D" relief on one side is not
                 reconstructed - see spec.md S3 reconstructability)
  - tab_small/tab_med/tab_large (canon 115/116/117, 1 instance each) - a
                 family of 3 flat rectangular cable-clip tabs sharing the same
                 6.9 mm height, differing only in width and thickness
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Params:
    # ---- block: canon 14, 4 instances ----
    block_w: float = 20.0     # X extent
    block_d: float = 12.0     # Y extent
    block_h: float = 40.0     # Z extent

    # ---- guide_pin: canon 20, 3 instances ----
    pin_d: float = 5.0        # pin diameter
    pin_len: float = 50.0     # pin length
    pin_flat_depth: float = 0.613   # depth of the flat "D" relief cut into the
                                     # cylindrical surface, measured from the
                                     # nominal radius (anti-rotation flat)
    pin_flat_len: float = 18.0      # length of the flat, measured back from
                                     # the pin's TOP end

    # ---- cable-clip tab family: canon 115 / 116 / 117, 1 instance each ----
    tab_h: float = 6.9        # shared height (Z), all 3 tabs
    tab_small_w: float = 19.2
    tab_small_t: float = 1.25
    tab_med_w: float = 19.4
    tab_med_t: float = 2.0
    tab_large_w: float = 19.8
    tab_large_t: float = 3.0

codex

I can still score exported STLs and run deterministic physical checks, but new STEP renders are unavailable in this sandbox; for visual context I’ll use existing render artifacts where present plus code/spec geometry evidence, and note the render failure in each final report.

def _check_feature_count(condition: dict, parts_by_name: dict[str, dict]) -> dict:
    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
    if "features" in inputs:
        names = [name for name in inputs.get("features", []) if name in parts_by_name]
    elif "part_name_prefix" in inputs:
        prefix = str(inputs["part_name_prefix"])
        names = [name for name in parts_by_name if name.startswith(prefix)]
    else:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "feature_count requires features or part_name_prefix")
    thresholds = condition.get("thresholds", {})
    expected = thresholds.get("expected_count")
    min_count = thresholds.get("min_count", expected)
    max_count = thresholds.get("max_count", expected)
    actual = len(names)
    passed = True
    if min_count is not None:
        passed = passed and actual >= int(min_count)
    if max_count is not None:
        passed = passed and actual <= int(max_count)
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if passed else "fail",
        f"feature count {actual}",
        {
            "actual_count": actual,
            "matched_features": sorted(names),
            "expected_count": int(expected) if expected is not None else None,
            "min_count": int(min_count) if min_count is not None else None,
            "max_count": int(max_count) if max_count is not None else None,
        },
    )


def _check_cylindrical_fit(condition: dict) -> dict:
    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
    try:
        pin_diameter = float(inputs.get("pin_diameter_mm"))
        hole_diameter = float(inputs.get("hole_diameter_mm"))
    except Exception:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "cylindrical_fit requires numeric pin_diameter_mm and hole_diameter_mm")
    clearance = hole_diameter - pin_diameter
    thresholds = condition.get("thresholds", {})
    min_clearance = float(thresholds.get("min_diameter_clearance_mm", 0.0))
    max_clearance = thresholds.get("max_diameter_clearance_mm")
    max_clearance = float(max_clearance) if max_clearance is not None else None
    passed = clearance >= min_clearance and (max_clearance is None or clearance <= max_clearance)
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if passed else "fail",
        f"diameter clearance {clearance:.4f} mm",
        {
            "pin_diameter_mm": pin_diameter,
            "hole_diameter_mm": hole_diameter,
            "diameter_clearance_mm": round(clearance, 6),
            "min_diameter_clearance_mm": min_clearance,
            "max_diameter_clearance_mm": max_clearance,
        },
    )


def _pair_distance(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
    pair: list[str],
) -> tuple[float | None, str | None]:
    if len(pair) != 2:
        return None, f"contact pair must contain exactly two part names: {pair}"
    tri_a, err_a = _load_part_triangles(parts_by_name, pair[0])
    tri_b, err_b = _load_part_triangles(parts_by_name, pair[1])
    if err_a or err_b:
        return None, "; ".join(err for err in (err_a, err_b) if err)
    assert tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None
    return _mesh_min_distance(tri_a, tri_b, assumptions), None


def _check_contact_graph(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
    expected = inputs.get("expected_contacts", [])
    forbidden = inputs.get("forbidden_contacts", [])
    thresholds = condition.get("thresholds", {})
    max_contact = float(thresholds.get("max_contact_distance_mm", 0.1))
    min_forbidden = float(thresholds.get("min_forbidden_clearance_mm", max_contact))
    edge_results: list[dict] = []
    failed: list[dict] = []

    for pair in expected:
        distance, error = _pair_distance(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions, pair)
        if error:
            return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", error)
        assert distance is not None
        ok = distance <= max_contact
        result = {
            "kind": "expected",
            "parts": pair,
            "distance_mm": round(float(distance), 6),
            "passed": ok,
        }
        edge_results.append(result)
        if not ok:
            failed.append(result)

    for pair in forbidden:
        distance, error = _pair_distance(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions, pair)
        if error:
            return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", error)
        assert distance is not None
        ok = distance >= min_forbidden
        result = {
            "kind": "forbidden",
            "parts": pair,
            "distance_mm": round(float(distance), 6),
            "passed": ok,
        }
        edge_results.append(result)
        if not ok:
            failed.append(result)

    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if not failed else "fail",
        f"contact graph checked {len(edge_results)} edges, {len(failed)} failed",
        {
            "edges": edge_results,
            "failed_edges": failed,
            "max_contact_distance_mm": max_contact,
            "min_forbidden_clearance_mm": min_forbidden,
        },
    )


def _check_assembly_component_count(condition: dict, assembly_report: dict | None, part_reports: list[dict]) -> dict:
    if assembly_report is None:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "no assembly STL supplied")
    if not assembly_report.get("loadable"):
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "assembly STL failed to load")

    expected = condition.get("thresholds", {}).get("expected_components")
    if expected is None:
        expected = len(part_reports)
    actual = int(assembly_report["metrics"]["connected_components"])
    status = "pass" if actual == int(expected) else "fail"
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        status,
        f"assembly connected components {actual} {'==' if status == 'pass' else '!='} expected {expected}",
        {"actual_components": actual, "expected_components": int(expected)},
    )

    condition: dict,
    status: str,
    detail: str,
    measurements: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
) -> dict:
    return {
        "id": condition.get("id", "unnamed_condition"),
        "category": condition.get("category", "physical_correctness"),
        "severity": condition.get("severity", "major"),
        "check": condition.get("check", "unknown"),
        "status": status,

def _check_assembly_sequence(
    condition: dict,
    assembly_report: dict | None,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    part_reports: list[dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    steps = condition.get("inputs", {}).get("steps", [])
    if not isinstance(steps, list) or not steps:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "assembly_sequence requires a non-empty steps list")

    step_results: list[dict] = []
    failed_steps: list[str] = []
    inconclusive_steps: list[str] = []
    for index, step in enumerate(steps):
        if not isinstance(step, dict):
            return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", f"assembly_sequence step {index} must be an object")
        subcondition = {
            "id": step.get("id", f"step_{index}"),
            "category": step.get("category", condition.get("category", "motion_path")),
            "severity": step.get("severity", condition.get("severity", "major")),
            "description": step.get("description", ""),
            "check": step.get("check"),
            "inputs": step.get("inputs", {}),
            "thresholds": step.get("thresholds", {}),
        }
        result = _run_condition(subcondition, assembly_report, parts_by_name, part_reports, assumptions)
        step_results.append(result)
        if result["status"] == "fail":
            failed_steps.append(result["id"])
        elif result["status"] == "inconclusive":
            inconclusive_steps.append(result["id"])

    if failed_steps:
        status = "fail"
        detail = f"assembly sequence failed at step(s): {', '.join(failed_steps)}"
    elif inconclusive_steps:
        status = "inconclusive"
        detail = f"assembly sequence inconclusive at step(s): {', '.join(inconclusive_steps)}"
    else:
        status = "pass"
        detail = f"assembly sequence passed {len(step_results)} steps"
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        status,
        detail,
        {
            "step_results": step_results,
            "failed_steps": failed_steps,
            "inconclusive_steps": inconclusive_steps,
        },
    )


def _run_condition(
    condition: dict,
    assembly_report: dict | None,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    part_reports: list[dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    check = condition.get("check")
    if check == "assembly_component_count":
        return _check_assembly_component_count(condition, assembly_report, part_reports)
    if check == "part_collision":
        return _check_part_collision(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "part_clearance":
        return _check_part_clearance(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "part_contact":
        return _check_part_contact(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "linear_motion_collision":
        return _check_linear_motion_collision(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "part_component_count":
        return _check_part_component_count(condition, parts_by_name)
    if check == "linear_motion_clearance":
        return _check_linear_motion_clearance(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "rotation_motion_collision":
        return _check_rotation_motion_collision(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "axis_alignment":
        return _check_axis_alignment(condition)
    if check == "relative_pose":
        return _check_relative_pose(condition, parts_by_name)
    if check == "opening_presence":
        return _check_opening_presence(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "clear_path_proxy":
        return _check_clear_path_proxy(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "vent_opening_proxy":
        return _check_vent_opening_proxy(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "vent_grid_open_area_proxy":
        return _check_vent_grid_open_area_proxy(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "feature_count":
        return _check_feature_count(condition, parts_by_name)
    if check == "cylindrical_fit":
        return _check_cylindrical_fit(condition)
    if check == "contact_graph":
        return _check_contact_graph(condition, parts_by_name, assumptions)
    if check == "assembly_sequence":
        return _check_assembly_sequence(condition, assembly_report, parts_by_name, part_reports, assumptions)
    return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", f"unsupported check: {check}")


def _combine_scores(
    baseline: dict,
    condition_results: list[dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    score = float(baseline["score"])
    hard_failures = list(baseline["hard_failures"])
    risk_factors = list(baseline["risk_factors"])
    cap = 10.0

    for result in condition_results:
        severity = result["severity"]
        cid = result["id"]
        if result["status"] == "fail":
            if severity == "critical":
                cap = min(cap, assumptions.critical_failure_cap)
                hard_failures.append(f"critical condition failed: {cid}")
                score = min(score, assumptions.critical_failure_cap)
            else:
                risk_factors.append(f"{severity} condition failed: {cid}")
                score -= _severity_penalty(severity, assumptions)
        elif result["status"] == "inconclusive":
            risk_factors.append(f"condition inconclusive: {cid}")
            score -= assumptions.inconclusive_penalty

    score = _round_score(min(score, cap))
    return {
        "score": score,
        "class": _class_for_score(score),
        "hard_failures": list(dict.fromkeys(hard_failures)),
        "risk_factors": list(dict.fromkeys(risk_factors)),
    }


def score_physical_correctness_project(
    assembly: str | os.PathLike | None = None,
    parts: Iterable[str | os.PathLike] | dict[str, str | os.PathLike] | None = None,
    conditions: list[dict] | None = None,
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions | None = None,
) -> dict:
    assumptions = assumptions or PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions()
    if isinstance(parts, dict):
        part_items = list(parts.items())
        part_paths = [path for _, path in part_items]
        part_names = [name for name, _ in part_items]
    else:
        part_paths = list(parts or [])
        part_names = [Path(path).stem for path in part_paths]

    baseline = score_usability_project(
        assembly=assembly,
        parts=part_paths,
        assumptions=UsabilityAssumptions(),
    )
    part_reports = baseline["parts"]
    parts_by_name = {name: report for name, report in zip(part_names, part_reports)}
    condition_results = [
        _run_condition(condition, baseline["assembly"], parts_by_name, part_reports, assumptions)
        for condition in (conditions or [])
    ]
    combined = _combine_scores(baseline, condition_results, assumptions)
    return {
        **combined,
        "metrics": baseline["metrics"],
        "condition_results": condition_results,
        "assembly": baseline["assembly"],
        "parts": part_reports,
        "part_names": part_names,
        "assumptions": {
            "physical_correctness": asdict(assumptions),
            "layer1_geometry": baseline["assumptions"],
        },
    }

    raw_parts = data.get("parts", {})
    if isinstance(raw_parts, dict):
        parts = {name: root / rel for name, rel in raw_parts.items()}
    else:

    )


def build_arg_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Score project physical correctness.")
    parser.add_argument("--assembly", help="assembled STL path")
    parser.add_argument("--parts", nargs="*", default=None, help="separate part STL paths")
    parser.add_argument("--condition-manifest", help="JSON condition manifest")
    parser.add_argument("--json-out", help="optional output JSON path")
    return parser


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    parser = build_arg_parser()
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    if bool(args.condition_manifest) == bool(args.assembly or args.parts):
        parser.error("choose exactly one input mode: --condition-manifest or --assembly/--parts")

    if args.condition_manifest:
        report = score_condition_manifest(args.condition_manifest)
    else:
        report = score_physical_correctness_project(args.assembly, args.parts or [])

    text = json.dumps(report, indent=2)
    if args.json_out:
        Path(args.json_out).write_text(text + "\n")
    print(text)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

import json
import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path

from cad_reconstruction_eval.physical_correctness_score import (
    score_condition_manifest,
    score_physical_correctness_project,
)
from tests.test_printability_score import _cube_triangles, _write_ascii_stl


def _translated(triangles, offset):
    ox, oy, oz = offset
    return [
        tuple((x + ox, y + oy, z + oz) for x, y, z in tri)
        for tri in triangles
    ]


class PhysicalCorrectnessScoreTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_bbox_overlap_condition_is_unsupported(self):
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            root = Path(tmp)
            body = root / "body.stl"
            lid = root / "lid.stl"
            assembly = root / "assembly.stl"
            _write_ascii_stl(body, _cube_triangles(size=10.0))
            _write_ascii_stl(lid, _translated(_cube_triangles(size=10.0), (5.0, 0.0, 0.0)))
            _write_ascii_stl(
                assembly,
                _cube_triangles(size=10.0)
                + _translated(_cube_triangles(size=10.0), (5.0, 0.0, 0.0)),
            )
            manifest = {
                "assembly": "assembly.stl",
                "parts": {"body": "body.stl", "lid": "lid.stl"},
                "conditions": [
                    {
                        "id": "body_lid_do_not_overlap",
                        "category": "interference_collision",
                        "severity": "critical",
                        "description": "Body and lid must not overlap substantially.",
                        "check": "part_bbox_overlap",
                        "inputs": {"part_a": "body", "part_b": "lid"},
                        "thresholds": {"max_overlap_ratio": 0.01},
                    }
                ],
            }
            manifest_path = root / "physical_conditions.json"
            manifest_path.write_text(json.dumps(manifest))

            report = score_condition_manifest(manifest_path)

            self.assertEqual(report["condition_results"][0]["status"], "inconclusive")
            self.assertIn("unsupported check: part_bbox_overlap", report["condition_results"][0]["detail"])
            self.assertIn("condition inconclusive: body_lid_do_not_overlap", report["risk_factors"])
            self.assertGreater(report["score"], 4.0)

def _rotate_triangles(
    triangles: np.ndarray,
    axis_point: np.ndarray,
    axis_direction: np.ndarray,
    angle_deg: float,
) -> np.ndarray:
    flat = triangles.reshape(-1, 3)
    rotated = _rotate_points(flat, axis_point, axis_direction, angle_deg)
    return rotated.reshape(triangles.shape)


def _line_to_line_distance(point_a: np.ndarray, direction_a: np.ndarray, point_b: np.ndarray, direction_b: np.ndarray) -> float:
    cross = np.cross(direction_a, direction_b)
    denom = float(np.linalg.norm(cross))
    delta = point_b - point_a
    if denom <= 1e-12:
        return float(np.linalg.norm(np.cross(delta, direction_a)))
    return abs(float(np.dot(delta, cross / denom)))


def _check_part_component_count(condition: dict, parts_by_name: dict[str, dict]) -> dict:
    name = condition.get("inputs", {}).get("part")
    if not name:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "part_component_count requires part")
    triangles, error = _load_part_triangles(parts_by_name, name)
    if error:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", error)
    assert triangles is not None
    tri = _triangle_metrics(triangles)
    topo = _topology_metrics(triangles, tri["areas"], PrintabilityAssumptions())
    expected = int(condition.get("thresholds", {}).get("expected_components", 1))
    actual = int(topo["connected_components"])
    status = "pass" if actual == expected else "fail"
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        status,
        f"part {name} connected components {actual} {'==' if status == 'pass' else '!='} expected {expected}",
        {"part": name, "actual_components": actual, "expected_components": expected},
    )


def _check_part_collision(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    name_a, name_b, tri_a, tri_b, error = _load_two_named_parts(condition, parts_by_name)
    if error is not None:
        return error
    assert name_a is not None and name_b is not None and tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None
    collides = _meshes_collide(tri_a, tri_b, assumptions)
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "fail" if collides else "pass",
        f"sampled mesh collision {'detected' if collides else 'not detected'}",
        {"part_a": name_a, "part_b": name_b, "collides": collides},
    )


def _check_part_clearance(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    name_a, name_b, tri_a, tri_b, error = _load_two_named_parts(condition, parts_by_name)
    if error is not None:
        return error
    assert name_a is not None and name_b is not None and tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None
    distance = _mesh_min_distance(tri_a, tri_b, assumptions)
    thresholds = condition.get("thresholds", {})
    min_clearance = float(thresholds.get("min_clearance_mm", 0.0))
    max_clearance = thresholds.get("max_clearance_mm")
    max_clearance = float(max_clearance) if max_clearance is not None else None
    passed = distance >= min_clearance and (max_clearance is None or distance <= max_clearance)
    if max_clearance is None:
        detail = f"minimum distance {distance:.4f} mm {'>=' if passed else '<'} minimum clearance {min_clearance:.4f} mm"
    else:
        detail = (
            f"minimum distance {distance:.4f} mm "
            f"{'within' if passed else 'outside'} clearance range {min_clearance:.4f}..{max_clearance:.4f} mm"
        )
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if passed else "fail",
        detail,
        {
            "part_a": name_a,
            "part_b": name_b,
            "min_distance_mm": round(float(distance), 6),
            "min_clearance_mm": min_clearance,
            "max_clearance_mm": max_clearance,
        },
    )


def _check_part_contact(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    name_a, name_b, tri_a, tri_b, error = _load_two_named_parts(condition, parts_by_name)
    if error is not None:
        return error
    assert name_a is not None and name_b is not None and tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None
    distance = _mesh_min_distance(tri_a, tri_b, assumptions)
    max_contact = float(condition.get("thresholds", {}).get("max_contact_distance_mm", 0.1))
    passed = distance <= max_contact
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if passed else "fail",
        f"minimum distance {distance:.4f} mm {'<=' if passed else '>'} contact threshold {max_contact:.4f} mm",
        {
            "part_a": name_a,
            "part_b": name_b,
            "min_distance_mm": round(float(distance), 6),
            "max_contact_distance_mm": max_contact,
        },
    )


def _check_linear_motion_collision(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,

 succeeded in 0ms:
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    name_a, name_b, tri_a, tri_b, error = _load_two_named_parts(
        condition, parts_by_name, key_a="moving_part", key_b="obstacle_part"
    )
    if error is not None:
        return error
    assert name_a is not None and name_b is not None and tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None
    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
    try:
        translation = np.asarray(inputs.get("translation"), dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "linear_motion_collision requires numeric translation")
    if translation.shape != (3,):
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "linear_motion_collision translation must be [x, y, z]")
    steps = int(inputs.get("steps", 8))
    if steps < 1:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "linear_motion_collision steps must be >= 1")

    for step in range(steps + 1):
        moved = tri_a + (translation * (step / steps))
        if _meshes_collide(moved, tri_b, assumptions):
            return _condition_result(
                condition,
                "fail",
                f"sampled motion path collides at step {step}/{steps}",
                {
                    "moving_part": name_a,
                    "obstacle_part": name_b,
                    "collides": True,
                    "first_collision_step": step,
                    "steps": steps,
                    "translation": [float(x) for x in translation],
                },
            )
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass",
        f"no sampled collision across {steps} linear motion steps",
        {
            "moving_part": name_a,
            "obstacle_part": name_b,
            "collides": False,
            "steps": steps,
            "translation": [float(x) for x in translation],
        },
    )


def _linear_motion_steps(condition: dict) -> tuple[np.ndarray | None, int | None, dict | None]:
    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
    translation, error = _parse_vector(inputs.get("translation"), "linear_motion translation")
    if error:
        return None, None, _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", error)
    steps = int(inputs.get("steps", 8))
    if steps < 1:
        return None, None, _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "linear motion steps must be >= 1")
    return translation, steps, None


def _check_linear_motion_clearance(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    name_a, name_b, tri_a, tri_b, error = _load_two_named_parts(
        condition, parts_by_name, key_a="moving_part", key_b="obstacle_part"
    )
    if error is not None:
        return error
    translation, steps, step_error = _linear_motion_steps(condition)
    if step_error is not None:
        return step_error
    assert name_a is not None and name_b is not None and tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None
    assert translation is not None and steps is not None
    min_clearance = float(condition.get("thresholds", {}).get("min_clearance_mm", 0.0))
    best = float("inf")
    best_step = 0
    for step in range(steps + 1):
        moved = tri_a + (translation * (step / steps))
        distance = _mesh_min_distance(moved, tri_b, assumptions)
        if distance < best:
            best = distance
            best_step = step
    passed = best >= min_clearance
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if passed else "fail",
        f"minimum path clearance {best:.4f} mm {'>=' if passed else '<'} required {min_clearance:.4f} mm",
        {
            "moving_part": name_a,
            "obstacle_part": name_b,
            "minimum_path_clearance_mm": round(float(best), 6),
            "minimum_clearance_step": best_step,
            "min_clearance_mm": min_clearance,
            "steps": steps,
            "translation": [float(x) for x in translation],
        },
    )


def _rotation_inputs(condition: dict) -> tuple[np.ndarray | None, np.ndarray | None, float | None, float | None, int | None, dict | None]:
    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
    axis_point, point_error = _parse_vector(inputs.get("axis_point"), "axis_point")
    if point_error:
        return None, None, None, None, None, _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", point_error)
    axis_direction, dir_error = _normalize_vector(inputs.get("axis_direction"))
    if dir_error:
        return None, None, None, None, None, _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", f"axis_direction {dir_error}")
    try:
        start = float(inputs.get("angle_start_deg", 0.0))
        end = float(inputs.get("angle_end_deg"))
    except Exception:
        return None, None, None, None, None, _condition_result(
            condition, "inconclusive", "rotation motion requires numeric angle_end_deg"
        )
    steps = int(inputs.get("steps", 8))
    if steps < 1:
        return None, None, None, None, None, _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", "rotation motion steps must be >= 1")
    return axis_point, axis_direction, start, end, steps, None


def _check_rotation_motion_collision(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
    assumptions: PhysicalCorrectnessAssumptions,
) -> dict:
    name_a, name_b, tri_a, tri_b, error = _load_two_named_parts(
        condition, parts_by_name, key_a="moving_part", key_b="obstacle_part"
    )
    if error is not None:
        return error
    axis_point, axis_direction, start, end, steps, input_error = _rotation_inputs(condition)
    if input_error is not None:
        return input_error
    assert name_a is not None and name_b is not None and tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None
    assert axis_point is not None and axis_direction is not None and start is not None and end is not None and steps is not None
    for step in range(steps + 1):
        angle = start + (end - start) * (step / steps)
        moved = _rotate_triangles(tri_a, axis_point, axis_direction, angle)
        if _meshes_collide(moved, tri_b, assumptions):
            return _condition_result(
                condition,
                "fail",
                f"sampled rotation path collides at step {step}/{steps}",
                {
                    "moving_part": name_a,
                    "obstacle_part": name_b,
                    "collides": True,
                    "first_collision_step": step,
                    "first_collision_angle_deg": round(float(angle), 6),
                    "steps": steps,
                },
            )
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass",
        f"no sampled collision across {steps} rotation steps",
        {"moving_part": name_a, "obstacle_part": name_b, "collides": False, "steps": steps},
    )


def _check_axis_alignment(condition: dict) -> dict:
    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
    axis_a = inputs.get("axis_a", {})
    axis_b = inputs.get("axis_b", {})
    point_a, err = _parse_vector(axis_a.get("point"), "axis_a.point")
    if err:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", err)
    point_b, err = _parse_vector(axis_b.get("point"), "axis_b.point")
    if err:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", err)
    direction_a, err = _normalize_vector(axis_a.get("direction"))
    if err:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", f"axis_a.direction {err}")
    direction_b, err = _normalize_vector(axis_b.get("direction"))
    if err:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", f"axis_b.direction {err}")
    assert point_a is not None and point_b is not None and direction_a is not None and direction_b is not None
    dot = abs(float(np.clip(np.dot(direction_a, direction_b), -1.0, 1.0)))
    angle = float(np.degrees(np.arccos(dot)))
    offset = _line_to_line_distance(point_a, direction_a, point_b, direction_b)
    thresholds = condition.get("thresholds", {})
    max_offset = float(thresholds.get("max_axis_offset_mm", 0.5))
    max_angle = float(thresholds.get("max_angle_deg", 2.0))
    passed = offset <= max_offset and angle <= max_angle
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if passed else "fail",
        f"axis offset {offset:.4f} mm, angle {angle:.4f} deg",
        {
            "axis_offset_mm": round(offset, 6),
            "axis_angle_deg": round(angle, 6),
            "max_axis_offset_mm": max_offset,
            "max_angle_deg": max_angle,
        },
    )


def _check_relative_pose(
    condition: dict,
    parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
) -> dict:
    name_a, name_b, tri_a, tri_b, error = _load_two_named_parts(condition, parts_by_name)
    if error is not None:
        return error
    axis, axis_error = _normalize_vector(condition.get("inputs", {}).get("axis"))
    if axis_error:
        return _condition_result(condition, "inconclusive", f"axis {axis_error}")
    assert name_a is not None and name_b is not None and tri_a is not None and tri_b is not None and axis is not None
    centroid_a = _mesh_centroid(tri_a)
    centroid_b = _mesh_centroid(tri_b)
    delta = float(np.dot(centroid_b - centroid_a, axis))
    thresholds = condition.get("thresholds", {})
    min_delta = thresholds.get("min_delta_mm")
    max_delta = thresholds.get("max_delta_mm")
    passed = True
    if min_delta is not None:
        passed = passed and delta >= float(min_delta)
    if max_delta is not None:
        passed = passed and delta <= float(max_delta)
    return _condition_result(
        condition,
        "pass" if passed else "fail",
        f"centroid delta along axis {delta:.4f} mm",
        {
            "part_a": name_a,
            "part_b": name_b,
            "centroid_delta_along_axis_mm": round(delta, 6),
            "min_delta_mm": float(min_delta) if min_delta is not None else None,
            "max_delta_mm": float(max_delta) if max_delta is not None else None,

codex
The helper is feasible as a proxy, not a full articulation solver: it can validate ball-vs-socket radial clearance/interference from explicit dimensions, but it cannot prove snap compliance, retention force, or swept rotation. I’m adding that one helper, documenting its limits, and using it only for the DUMMY frame’s exposed ball/socket radii.

+def _check_spherical_fit(condition: dict) -> dict:
+    inputs = condition.get("inputs", {})
+    try:
+        if "ball_diameter_mm" in inputs:
+            ball_radius = float(inputs["ball_diameter_mm"]) / 2.0
+        else:
+            ball_radius = float(inputs.get("ball_radius_mm"))
+        if "socket_diameter_mm" in inputs:
+            socket_radius = float(inputs["socket_diameter_mm"]) / 2.0
+        else:
+            socket_radius = float(inputs.get("socket_radius_mm"))
+    except Exception:
+        return _condition_result(
+            condition,
+            "inconclusive",
+            "spherical_fit requires ball_radius_mm/socket_radius_mm or ball_diameter_mm/socket_diameter_mm",
+        )
+    radial_clearance = socket_radius - ball_radius
+    thresholds = condition.get("thresholds", {})
+    min_clearance = float(thresholds.get("min_radial_clearance_mm", 0.0))
+    max_clearance = thresholds.get("max_radial_clearance_mm")
+    max_clearance = float(max_clearance) if max_clearance is not None else None
+    passed = radial_clearance >= min_clearance and (max_clearance is None or radial_clearance <= max_clearance)
+    return _condition_result(
+        condition,
+        "pass" if passed else "fail",
+        f"radial clearance {radial_clearance:.4f} mm",
+        {
+            "ball_radius_mm": ball_radius,
+            "socket_radius_mm": socket_radius,
+            "radial_clearance_mm": round(radial_clearance, 6),
+            "diameter_clearance_mm": round(radial_clearance * 2.0, 6),
+            "min_radial_clearance_mm": min_clearance,
+            "max_radial_clearance_mm": max_clearance,
+            "proxy_limitation": "checks only explicit ball/socket size compatibility; it does not validate socket mouth geometry, snap compliance, retention force, or swept articulation clearance",
+        },
+    )
+
+
 def _pair_distance(
     condition: dict,
     parts_by_name: dict[str, dict],
@@ -1213,6 +1252,8 @@
         return _check_feature_count(condition, parts_by_name)
     if check == "cylindrical_fit":
